Testing What AI Can Actually See and Understand 96

Papers build benchmarks to test whether vision-language models can handle real images, spatial reasoning, and visual understanding tasks that humans find natural — from design aesthetics to ancient pottery.

ICLR 2026

Seeing Through Deception: Uncovering Misleading Creator Intent in Multimodal News with Vision-Language Models

Problem. Misinformation detection relies on identifying both factual inaccuracies and the misleading narratives deliberately embedded by creators, but vision-language models fail to understand creator intent in multimodal content.
Approach. DeceptionDecoded introduces a large-scale benchmark of 12,000 image-caption pairs grounded in trustworthy articles, using an intent-guided simulation framework to model both desired influence and execution plans, then evaluates state-of-the-art VLMs on three intent-centric tasks.
Contribution. Demonstrates that current VLMs struggle with intent reasoning and rely on shallow cues, establishing DeceptionDecoded as a foundation for developing intent-aware models for multimodal misinformation detection.
vision-language modelsintent detectionmultimodal analysisbenchmark creation

ICLR 2026

Video-LevelGauge: Investigating Contextual Positional Bias in Video Language Models.

Problem. Existing video language model benchmarks assess overall performance across entire video sequences but overlook positional biases, a critical yet underexplored aspect of LVLM behavior.
Approach. We introduce Video-LevelGauge, a dedicated benchmark with standardized probes and customized contextual setups that flexibly control context length, probe position, and contextual types, combined with statistical measures and bias pattern recognition.
Contribution. Evaluation of 27 state-of-the-art LVLMs reveals significant positional biases in open-source models (head/neighbor-content preferences) while commercial models like Gemini 2.5 Pro show consistent performance across video sequences.
positional bias detectioncontextual probingbias pattern recognitionvideo understanding evaluation

ICLR 2026

VUDG: A Dataset for Video Understanding Domain Generalization

Problem. The robustness of video understanding models to domain shifts encountered in real-world applications remains critical yet underexplored, limiting practical reliability.
Approach. We introduce VUDG, a dataset with 11 distinct domains covering three types of domain shifts while maintaining semantic consistency, plus a multi-expert progressive annotation framework for efficient labeling.
Contribution. Evaluation of 9 representative LVLMs and traditional VQA methods shows most models suffer performance degradation under domain shifts, highlighting challenges in robustness.
domain generalizationvideo question answeringdomain shift evaluationvideo understanding

ICLR 2026

IWR-Bench: Can LVLMs reconstruct interactive webpage from a user interaction video?

Problem. Existing webpage-to-code benchmarks focus on static screenshot-to-code tasks, overlooking the dynamic interactions fundamental to real-world web applications.
Approach. IWR-Bench introduces a benchmark for evaluating LVLMs in interactive webpage reconstruction from video, comprising 113 curated tasks with 1,001 actions from real-world websites and an agent-as-a-judge evaluation framework.
Contribution. Experiments reveal that the best LVLM model achieves only 36.35% overall score with functional correctness (24.39%) lagging visual fidelity (64.25%), highlighting critical limitations in reasoning about temporal dynamics.
video understandingcode generationwebpage reconstruction
ICLR 2026

IV-Bench: A Benchmark for Image-Grounded Video Perception and Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs

Problem. Multimodal LLM evaluation frameworks overlook the significant role of image context in video comprehension, focusing mainly on image reasoning or general video understanding.
Approach. IV-Bench provides 966 videos paired with 2,560 image-text queries across 13 tasks (7 perception, 6 reasoning) and 5 categories, evaluating MLLMs on image-grounded video perception.
Contribution. Current models achieve at most 28.9% accuracy, revealing key performance factors including inference pattern, frame number, and resolution.
multimodal evaluationvideo understandingbenchmark design
ICLR 2026

Do 3D Large Language Models Really Understand 3D Spatial Relationships?

Problem. Existing 3D-LLM evaluation benchmarks do not reliably measure genuine 3D spatial reasoning, as text-only models exploit textual shortcuts rather than demonstrating 3D understanding.
Approach. Real-3DQA provides rigorous evaluation that filters out easy-to-guess questions and introduces a structured taxonomy for assessing 3D reasoning; 3D-reweighted training uses negative samples via explicit 3D-relation alignment.
Contribution. Real-3DQA confirms existing 3D-LLMs struggle with spatial relationships when simple cues are removed, and 3D-reweighted training substantially enhances performance.
spatial reasoning benchmark3D-relation alignmentnegative sampling
ICLR 2026

Human-MME: A Holistic Evaluation Benchmark for Human-Centric Multimodal Large Language Models

Problem. Existing MLLMs evaluation benchmarks lack comprehensive assessment of human-centric scene understanding, failing to capture both granular perception and higher-dimensional causal reasoning about people.
Approach. Human-MME provides rigorously curated evaluation across 4 primary visual domains with 15 secondary domains, featuring 19,945 image question pairs with progressive evaluation dimensions from granular to causal reasoning.
Contribution. Human-MME's high-quality annotations with diverse data paradigms and 43 sub-fields enable precise assessment of MLLMs on human-centric scene understanding spanning multi-person and multi-image reasoning.
multimodal evaluationhuman-centric understandingcausal reasoning
ICLR 2026

RAVENEA: A Benchmark for Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Visual Culture Understanding

Problem. Multimodal models struggle with cultural nuances and their application in retrieval-augmented generation remains underexplored.
Approach. Introduces RAVENEA, a benchmark with culture-focused VQA and culture-informed image captioning tasks, integrating over 11,396 Wikipedia documents.
Contribution. Shows VLMs augmented with culture-aware retrieval outperform non-augmented counterparts by averaging +6% on cVQA and +11% on cIC.
retrieval-augmented generationvision-language modelscultural grounding
ICLR 2026

SCoT: Teaching 3D-LLMs to Think Spatially with Million-scale CoT Annotations

Problem. 3D Large Language Models typically lack explicit reasoning processes in training data, limiting complex spatial reasoning and task planning capabilities.
Approach. SCoT annotates a million-scale Chain-of-Thought dataset across three levels: spatial perception, analysis, and planning, with intermediate reasoning grounded in scene cues.
Contribution. Shows CoT supervision greatly benefits complex analysis and planning but can induce hallucinations in simple perception tasks, highlighting nuanced challenges of scene-grounded reasoning.
chain-of-thought reasoningspatial reasoning3d understanding
ICLR 2026

TABLET: A Large-Scale Dataset for Robust Visual Table Understanding

Problem. Current visual table understanding benchmarks rely on synthetic renderings lacking complexity of real-world tables and offer no access to underlying serialized data for reformulation.
Approach. TABLET provides 4 million examples across 21 tasks grounded in 2 million unique tables with 88% original visualizations; introduces VisualTableQA requiring joint visual and tabular reasoning.
Contribution. Fine-tuning vision-language models on TABLET improves performance on seen/unseen VTU tasks while increasing robustness on real-world table visualizations.
visual table understandingvision-language modelsmultimodal reasoning
ICLR 2026

Do Vision-Language Models Respect Contextual Integrity in Location Disclosure?

Problem. Vision-language models can geolocate images with street-level precision; widely accessible models enable geolocation from casually shared photos.
Approach. VLM-GEOPRIVACY benchmark challenges VLMs to interpret social norms and determine appropriate location disclosure levels.
Contribution. 14 leading VLMs poorly align with human privacy expectations, over-disclosing in sensitive contexts and vulnerable to prompt attacks.
privacy benchmarksvision-language modelscontextual integrity
ICLR 2026

OmniSpatial: Towards Comprehensive Spatial Reasoning Benchmark for Vision Language Models

Problem. Vision-language models struggle with comprehensive spatial reasoning beyond basic left/right/near/far distinctions, lacking benchmarks for complex spatial logic and perspective-taking.
Approach. OmniSpatial provides a grounded benchmark with 8.4K QA pairs spanning four categories: dynamic reasoning, complex logic, spatial interaction, and perspective-taking, grounded in cognitive psychology.
Contribution. Reveals significant limitations of current VLMs in comprehensive spatial reasoning and proposes PointGraph and SpatialCoT strategies to improve reasoning.
benchmark constructionspatial reasoningchain-of-thought prompting
ICLR 2026

MME-Unify: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Unified Multimodal Understanding and Generation Models

Problem. Existing benchmarks lack unified evaluation for multimodal comprehension, generation, and mixed-modality generation capabilities in U-MLLMs.
Approach. Create MME-Unify benchmark curating diverse tasks from 12 datasets with standardized formats and metrics, design five mixed-modality subtasks for unified evaluation.
Contribution. Evaluation of 12 U-MLLMs reveals significant improvements needed in instruction following and image generation quality, providing comprehensive assessment framework.
multimodal evaluationbenchmark designunified assessmentmixed-modality tasks

ICLR 2026

Spatial CAPTCHA: Generatively Benchmarking Spatial Reasoning for Human-Machine Differentiation

Problem. Modern MLLMs have undermined traditional text-recognition CAPTCHAs; current defenses remain vulnerable to AI advances in 2D image understanding.
Approach. Spatial CAPTCHA leverages fundamental differences in spatial reasoning between humans and MLLMs, generating dynamic questions requiring geometric reasoning, perspective-taking, occlusion handling, and mental rotation via procedural generation.
Contribution. Humans vastly outperform 10 state-of-the-art MLLMs; best model achieves only 31.0% Pass@1 accuracy, confirming effectiveness as both security mechanism and diagnostic tool.
spatial reasoningprocedural generationconstraint-based difficultyhuman-machine differentiation
ICLR 2026

Theory of Space: Can Foundation Models Construct Spatial Beliefs through Active Exploration?

Problem. Multimodal foundation models have not been systematically evaluated for actively acquiring information through self-directed exploration under partial observability.
Approach. The authors propose Theory of Space as an evaluation framework where agents perform curiosity-driven exploration and reveal their internal spatial beliefs as cognitive maps at each step.
Contribution. Evaluation of state-of-the-art models reveals critical bottlenecks in active exploration and spatial reasoning, with performance degrading significantly when agents must autonomously gather information.
spatial reasoningactive learningbenchmark design
ICLR 2026

ScaleLong: A Multi-Timescale Benchmark for Long Video Understanding

Problem. Current long-video understanding benchmarks entangle timescales with video content, preventing clear assessment of multimodal LLM performance across different temporal scales.
Approach. ScaleLong embeds questions targeting four hierarchical timescales within the same video, enabling direct performance comparison across timescales on identical content.
Contribution. Evaluation of 22 MLLMs reveals a U-shaped performance trend with higher accuracy at shortest and longest timescales, providing a fine-grained multi-timescale benchmark.
multi-timescale evaluationlong video understandingbenchmark design
ICLR 2026

Dataset Color Quantization: A Training-Oriented Framework for Dataset-Level Compression

Problem. Large-scale image datasets have high storage demands due to color-space redundancy, and existing dataset compression approaches discard entire samples while ignoring redundancy within images.
Approach. Dataset Color Quantization (DCQ) compresses datasets by reducing color-space redundancy while preserving semantically important colors and structural details through palette consistency and model-perception guidance.
Contribution. DCQ significantly improves training performance under aggressive compression on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, and ImageNet-1K, offering scalable dataset-level storage reduction.
color quantizationpalette optimizationdataset compression
ICLR 2026

FETAL-GAUGE: A BENCHMARK FOR ASSESSING VISION-LANGUAGE MODELS IN FETAL ULTRASOUND

Problem. No standardized benchmark exists to evaluate vision-language models on fetal ultrasound interpretation despite high clinical demand and VLM promise for multi-task ultrasound analysis.
Approach. Fetal-Gauge provides 42,000+ images and 93,000 question-answer pairs spanning anatomical plane identification, visual grounding, fetal orientation, view conformity, and clinical diagnosis.
Contribution. Systematic evaluation reveals the best-performing VLM achieves only 55% accuracy far below clinical requirements, identifying critical limitations in domain-specific VLM adaptation.
visual question answeringbenchmark curationmedical imaging
ICLR 2026

From EduVisBench to EduVisAgent: A Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Reasoning-Driven Pedagogical Visualization

Problem. Foundation models struggle to generate pedagogically effective visual explanations for STEM education because they focus primarily on textual reasoning and overlook the role of structured visualizations in supporting conceptual understanding.
Approach. EduVisAgent is a multi-agent framework coordinating specialized agents for instructional planning, reasoning decomposition, metacognitive prompting, and visualization design, evaluated on EduVisBench with multi-domain STEM problems.
Contribution. EduVisAgent achieves 40.2% improvement over baselines and delivers more educationally aligned visualizations grounded in pedagogical theory.
multi-agent frameworkvisual reasoningpedagogical design
ICLR 2026

Children's Intelligence Tests Pose Challenges for MLLMs? KidGym: A 2D Grid-Based Reasoning Benchmark for MLLMs

Problem. Multimodal LLM evaluation lacks assessment of cognitive capabilities like execution, perception reasoning, learning, memory, and planning that mirror children's development.
Approach. KidGym is a 2D grid-based benchmark with 12 tasks inspired by Wechsler Intelligence Scales, with random object layouts and customizable difficulty levels.
Contribution. Evaluation of SOTA MLLMs reveals significant limitations and provides actionable insights into model capabilities.
grid-based reasoningcognitive benchmarkingmultimodal evaluation
ICLR 2026

Can Vision-Language Models Answer Face to Face Questions in the Real-World?

Problem. Existing vision-language and audio models cannot reliably converse with users in real-time about live scenes, despite advances in individual modalities.
Approach. The work introduces the Interactive Video Dataset (IVD) that assesses how well existing models support real-time question-answering based on camera and audio input, and shows fine-tuning on this data reduces performance gaps.
Contribution. IVD reveals that existing models fall far behind human performance and identifies main sources of performance gaps, while showing fine-tuning can significantly reduce the gap.
interactive video understandingmultimodal benchmarkingreal-time reasoningfine-tuning

ICLR 2026

VaseVQA-3D: Benchmarking 3D VLMs on Ancient Greek Pottery

Problem. Existing Vision-Language Models lack targeted training data and domain knowledge to effectively analyze specialized cultural heritage domains like 3D ancient Greek pottery artifacts.
Approach. VaseVQA-3D provides a specialized dataset of 664 ancient Greek vase 3D models with question-answer pairs, and VaseVLM applies domain-adaptive training to improve performance on this cultural heritage task.
Contribution. VaseVLM-7B-RL achieves 12.8% improvement in R@1 accuracy and 6.6% improvement in lexical similarity on the VaseVQA-3D dataset compared to strongest baselines.
domain-adaptive training3d visual question answeringvision-language models
ICLR 2026

IndicVisionBench: Benchmarking Cultural and Multilingual Understanding in VLMs

Problem. Existing vision-language model benchmarks remain Western-centric, leaving open questions about VLM performance in culturally diverse and multilingual contexts.
Approach. IndicVisionBench provides a large-scale benchmark centered on the Indian subcontinent covering English and 10 Indian languages across OCR, Multimodal Machine Translation, and Visual Question Answering tasks.
Contribution. Evaluation of 8 models reveals substantial performance gaps, demonstrating significant limitations of current VLMs in culturally diverse contexts.
multilingual benchmarkcultural diversityvision-language models
ICLR 2026

A Structured, Tagged, and Localized Visual Question Answering Dataset with Full Sentence Answers and Scene Graphs for Chest X-ray Images

Problem. Existing chest X-ray VQA datasets are constrained by simplistic answer formats, lack localization annotations, and absence of structured semantic tags.
Approach. CXR-QBA derives a large-scale dataset from MIMIC-CXR with 42 million QA-pairs using LLM-based information extraction from radiology reports to create scene graphs with structured tags.
Contribution. The dataset provides 31M pre-training and 7.5M fine-tuning grade QA-pairs with multi-granular answers and bounding boxes, the largest and most sophisticated VQA dataset for chest X-rays.
visual question answeringinformation extractionscene graphs
ICLR 2026

CubeBench: Diagnosing Interactive, Long-Horizon Physical Intelligence under Partial Observations

Problem. LLM agents lack robust spatial mental models for physical-world deployment, struggling with spatial reasoning, long-horizon state tracking, and exploration under partial observation.
Approach. Introduce CubeBench, a three-tiered diagnostic framework using Rubik's Cube tasks progressing from full symbolic information to partial visual data.
Contribution. Reveals uniform 0.00% pass rate on long-horizon tasks, exposing fundamental failures in long-term planning and identifying cognitive bottlenecks.
diagnostic benchmarkspatial reasoninglong-horizon planning
ICLR 2026

SpatialViz-Bench: A Cognitively-Grounded Benchmark for Diagnosing Spatial Visualization in MLLMs

Problem. Multi-modal benchmarks assess reasoning on visible information but inadequately evaluate spatial visualization, the ability to infer unseen relationships through mental rotation and manipulation.
Approach. Introduces SpatialViz-Bench with 12 tasks across 4 spatial sub-abilities comprising 1,180 programmatically generated problems, a scalable framework ensuring fair evaluations without data contamination.
Contribution. Evaluation of 27 MLLMs reveals wide performance variations and counter-intuitive finding that CoT prompting paradoxically degrades accuracy on open-source models.
spatial visualization benchmarkingprogrammatic task generationmultimodal evaluation
ICLR 2026

UrbanFeel：A Comprehensive Benchmark for Temporal and Perceptual Understanding of City Scenes through Human Perspective

Problem. Multimodal models lack systematic evaluation of temporal evolution and subjective perception of urban environments aligned with human perception, limiting understanding of urban development impacts.
Approach. Proposes UrbanFeel with 14.3K visual questions across three dimensions (Static Scene Perception, Temporal Change, Subjective Environmental Perception) from 11 global cities.
Contribution. Evaluation of 20 MLLMs shows Gemini-2.5 Pro achieves best performance approaching human expert levels, revealing models perform well on scene understanding but struggle with subjective environmental perception.
multimodal benchmarkingurban understandingtemporal perception
ICLR 2026

RobotArena $\infty$: Unlimited Robot Benchmarking via Real-to-Sim Translation

Problem. Real-world testing of robot policies is labor-intensive, slow, unsafe at scale, and difficult to reproduce, while simulation benchmarks train and test within the same synthetic domains, unable to assess vision-language-action models trained on real data.
Approach. Introduces RobotArena∞, a benchmarking framework converting video demonstrations to simulated counterparts via vision-language models and 2D-to-3D generative modeling, with both automated and human preference evaluation.
Contribution. Overcomes limitations of real-world and simulation-only benchmarks by enabling large-scale VLA evaluation with online human feedback for nuanced execution quality judgments.
sim-to-real translationvideo generationpreference evaluation
ICLR 2026

MCIF: Multimodal Crosslingual Instruction-Following Benchmark from Scientific Talks

Problem. Existing benchmarks fail to jointly evaluate multimodal LLMs across languages, modalities, and task complexity, limiting comprehensive assessment of crosslingual and multimodal capabilities.
Approach. MCIF introduces a human-annotated benchmark from scientific talks spanning three modalities (speech, vision, text), four languages (English, German, Italian, Chinese), and four macro-tasks (recognition, translation, question answering, summarization).
Contribution. Systematic evaluation of 23 models reveals universal challenges across modalities and languages, indicating substantial room for improvement in future MLLMs.
multimodal evaluationcrosslingual assessmenthuman annotation
ICLR 2026

Can Vision–Language Models Assess Graphic Design Aesthetics? A Benchmark, Evaluation, and Dataset Perspective.

Problem. Assessing aesthetic quality of graphic design remains underexplored in vision-language models due to narrow benchmarks, limited VLM comparisons, and scarcity of training data.
Approach. AesEval-Bench spans four dimensions with twelve indicators and three quantifiable tasks; constructs training dataset via human-guided VLM labeling with indicator-grounded reasoning.
Contribution. Establishes first systematic framework for aesthetic assessment in graphic design; reveals clear performance gaps of proprietary, open-source, and reasoning-augmented VLMs.
aesthetic evaluationdesign benchmarkvisions language models
ICLR 2026

FRIEDA: Benchmarking Multi-Step Cartographic Reasoning in Vision-Language Models

Problem. Cartographic reasoning—interpreting geographic relationships by aligning legends, scales, directions, and geometries—remains largely unevaluated despite its critical importance for disaster response and urban planning.
Approach. FRIEDA is a benchmark sourcing real map images from documents in various domains, targeting topological, metric, and directional spatial relations through multi-step inference questions.
Contribution. Evaluates eleven state-of-the-art LVLMs and reveals that models struggle with complex cartographic reasoning requiring cross-map grounding.
vision-language modelsbenchmark constructionspatial reasoningmulti-step inference
ICLR 2026

M3CoTBench: Benchmark Chain-of-Thought of MLLMs in Medical Image Understanding

Problem. Existing benchmarks for medical image understanding focus only on final answers, ignoring the reasoning path that clinicians need to trust AI diagnostic assistance.
Approach. We introduce M3CoTBench with 24 examination types, 13 tasks at varying difficulty, and CoT-specific metrics (correctness, efficiency, impact, consistency) tailored to clinical reasoning.
Contribution. The benchmark reveals current limitations of MLLMs in generating reliable and clinically interpretable reasoning for medical images.
chain-of-thought evaluationmedical image benchmarkingmultimodal llm assessment
ICLR 2026

Read the Room: Video Social Reasoning with Mental-Physical Causal Chains

Problem. Inferring others' mental states from subtle social cues (social reasoning) is a hallmark of human intelligence but remains a major challenge for AI, with existing datasets limited in complexity and mental state coverage.
Approach. Introduces R3-Bench evaluation benchmark with fine-grained annotations of belief, intent, desire, emotion and causal chains, plus R3-FDT large-scale training set generated through automated pipeline with same structure.
Contribution. Shows SOTA LVLMs fall far short of human-level reasoning on R3-Bench; fine-tuning 7B model with GRPO on R3-FDT achieves notable improvements across multiple social reasoning benchmarks.
social reasoningreinforcement learningmultimodal learning
ICLR 2026

LLMs as Rules Oracles: Exploring Real-World Multimodal Reasoning in Tabletop Strategy Game Environments

Problem. Multimodal reasoning on unfamiliar tabletop strategy games requires comprehending visual scenes and integrating heterogeneous rules, but evaluating this foundational reasoning is understudied.
Approach. LudoBench benchmark evaluates vision-language models on three cumulative capabilities: Environment Perception, Heterogeneous Rules Integration, and Short-horizon Optimization on mainstream strategy games.
Contribution. Shows frontier LMs achieve only ~68% on simple perception and <10% on situated multi-step comprehension, revealing failures in cross-modal reference knowledge and application.
multimodal reasoningtabletop gamesevaluation benchmark
ICLR 2026

STAR-Bench: Probing Deep Spatio-Temporal Reasoning as Audio 4D Intelligence

Problem. Audio benchmarks focus on semantics recoverable from text captions, masking deficits in fine-grained perceptual reasoning over sound and space.
Approach. STAR-Bench combines foundational acoustic perception with holistic spatio-temporal reasoning tasks synthesized procedurally and via physics simulation.
Contribution. Induces large accuracy drops (-31.5% temporal, -35.2% spatial) compared to caption-only answering, revealing gaps between models and human performance.
audio benchmarkingspatio-temporal reasoningperceptual evaluation
ICLR 2026

SpaCE-Eval: A Benchmark for Real-World Multi-Modal Reasoning

Problem. Evaluating multimodal LLMs remains incomplete; current benchmarks do not comprehensively assess spatial reasoning, commonsense knowledge, and environment interaction in real-world scenarios.
Approach. Introduces SpaCE-Eval, a visual-question-answering benchmark with human-produced diagrams testing spatial reasoning, commonsense physics, and environmental interaction through diagram-question pairs refined via rigorous pipeline.
Contribution. Demonstrates that leading MLLMs significantly underperform on spatial reasoning, indicating substantial enhancement necessary for real-world environmental understanding.
visual question answeringspatial reasoningbenchmark designmultimodal evaluation
ICLR 2026

VideoMathQA: Benchmarking Mathematical Reasoning via Multimodal Understanding in Video

Problem. Mathematical reasoning in video requires interpreting fine-grained visual information, reading text, and integrating spoken cues over extended time; existing benchmarks lack this complexity.
Approach. Introduces VideoMathQA benchmark with 920 man-hours of expert annotation spanning 10 mathematical domains and 10-second to 1-hour videos; includes multi-step reasoning annotations for fine-grained diagnosis.
Contribution. Establishes evaluation framework for temporally extended cross-modal mathematical reasoning; demonstrates significant challenge for MLLMs including closed and open-source models.
video understandingmathematical reasoningmultimodal reasoningbenchmark design
ICLR 2026

JointAVBench: A Benchmark for Joint Audio-Visual Reasoning Evaluation

Problem. Existing audio-visual benchmarks lack strict correlation, diverse audio types, and varying scene spans necessary for comprehensive Omni-LLM evaluation.
Approach. JointAVBench with automated pipeline leveraging vision/audio/general LLMs to synthesize questions and answers requiring joint audio-visual understanding across five cognitive dimensions.
Contribution. Shows best Omni-LLM achieves 62.6% accuracy outperforming uni-modal baselines but revealing substantial room for improvement in cross-scene reasoning.
benchmark constructionmultimodal evaluationsynthetic question generation
ICLR 2026

OCR-Reasoning Benchmark: Unveiling the True Capabilities of MLLMs in Complex Text-Rich Image Reasoning

Problem. Existing text-rich image understanding benchmarks lack systematic evaluation of MLLMs' reasoning capabilities in complex visual-textual scenarios and provide only final answers without step-by-step reasoning traces.
Approach. OCR-Reasoning provides 1,069 human-annotated examples with detailed step-by-step reasoning traces spanning 6 reasoning abilities and 18 practical tasks, enabling holistic evaluation of both answers and reasoning processes.
Contribution. Comprehensive evaluation shows advanced MLLMs exhibit substantial difficulties in text-rich reasoning, with none achieving 50% accuracy, revealing an urgent gap to address.
benchmark designmultimodal evaluationreasoning assessmenthuman annotation
ICLR 2026

Image Quality Assessment for Embodied AI

Problem. Traditional Image Quality Assessment methods predict human preferences but cannot assess image usability for embodied AI tasks.
Approach. Constructs perception-cognition-decision-execution pipeline with Embodied-IQA database containing 30k image pairs and 5m annotations via VLMs and real robots.
Contribution. Establishes first IQA benchmark for embodied AI, demonstrating need for quality indicators tailored to robot perception under distortions.
quality assessmentembodied AIbenchmarking
ICLR 2026

LiveWeb-IE: A Benchmark For Online Web Information Extraction

Problem. Traditional web information extraction evaluation relies on offline benchmarks from HTML snapshots at single time points, failing to account for the temporally evolving nature of live websites.
Approach. LiveWeb-IE introduces a benchmark for evaluating WIE systems against live websites with natural language queries requiring information extraction of various data categories, plus Visual Grounding Scraper that mimics human visual reasoning.
Contribution. The benchmark enables granular assessment of WIE systems across complexity levels, with VGS showing effectiveness across diverse models in extracting live web information.
web scrapinginformation extractionbenchmark designagentic frameworks
ICLR 2026

ExpVid: A Benchmark for Experiment Video Understanding & Reasoning

Problem. Existing benchmarks fail to capture the fine-grained and long-horizon nature of authentic laboratory work, limiting evaluation of MLLMs on scientific experiment understanding.
Approach. ExpVid benchmark curates experiment videos with three-level task hierarchy: fine-grained perception, procedural understanding, and scientific reasoning linking experiments to conclusions.
Contribution. 3.6K condition sets across six tasks and domains with 20K fine-grained annotations reveal that MLLMs excel at coarse recognition but struggle with details and high-order reasoning.
vision-language modelsbenchmark curationmulti-level annotation
ICLR 2026

FLUX-Reason-6M & PRISM-Bench: A Million-Scale Text-to-Image Reasoning Dataset and Comprehensive Benchmark

Problem. Open-source text-to-image models lack large-scale reasoning-focused datasets and comprehensive evaluation benchmarks, resulting in a performance gap compared to closed-source systems.
Approach. FLUX-Reason-6M provides 6 million FLUX-generated images with 20 million bilingual descriptions organized by six characteristics (Imagination, Entity, Text rendering, Style, Affection, Composition) and Generation Chain-of-Thought; PRISM-Bench offers seven evaluation tracks including a Long Text challenge.
Contribution. FLUX-Reason-6M and PRISM-Bench enable training and evaluation of open-source text-to-image models with comprehensive reasoning assessment across 19 leading models.
text-to-image datasetgeneration chain-of-thoughtmultimodal evaluation benchmark
ICLR 2026

Benchmarking Large Vision-Language Models on Fine-Grained Image Tasks: A Comprehensive Evaluation

Problem. Current large vision-language models are evaluated on broad tasks, but their performance on fine-grained image understanding tasks remains largely unexplored.
Approach. FG-BMK comprises 1.01 million questions and 0.28 million images, systematically evaluating LVLMs from human-oriented and machine-oriented perspectives across semantic recognition and fine-grained feature representation.
Contribution. FG-BMK reveals key findings on training paradigms, modality alignment, perturbation susceptibility, and fine-grained reasoning impacts on LVLMs, providing guidance for future model development.
fine-grained vision benchmarkmulti-perspective evaluationsystematic lvlm analysis
ICLR 2026

TPRU: Advancing Temporal and Procedural Understanding in Large Multimodal Models

Problem. Multimodal large language models lack the ability to understand temporal and procedural visual data, limiting their deployment in embodied AI applications like robotic manipulation.
Approach. TPRU is a large-scale dataset with three complementary temporal reasoning tasks (Temporal Reordering, Next-Frame Prediction, Previous-Frame Review) combined with RL fine-tuning specifically targeting resource-efficient model enhancement.
Contribution. TPRU-7B accuracy improved from 50.33% to 75.70% on TPRU-Test, achieving state-of-the-art results that significantly outperform GPT-4o.
dataset constructiontemporal reasoningreinforcement learningmultimodal learning
ICLR 2026

Everything in Its Place: Benchmarking Spatial Intelligence of Text-to-Image Models

Problem. Text-to-image models fail handling complex spatial relationships; current benchmarks overlook spatial reasoning due to short or information-sparse prompt design.
Approach. Introduces SpatialGenEval benchmark with 1,230 long information-dense prompts across 25 scenes covering 10 spatial sub-domains; constructs SpatialT2I dataset with 15,400 text-image pairs preserving consistency and information density.
Contribution. Reveals higher-order spatial reasoning as primary bottleneck; fine-tuned models show consistent gains (+4.2% to +5.7%) with more realistic spatial relations.
spatial reasoning evaluationinformation-dense promptsfine-tuning
ICLR 2026

GTR-Bench: Evaluating Geo-Temporal Reasoning in Vision-Language Models

Problem. Vision-language models cannot effectively reason about moving targets across large-scale camera networks by integrating geographic maps and multiple video perspectives simultaneously.
Approach. GTR-Bench introduces a benchmark that combines geographic and temporal reasoning with non-overlapping field-of-view videos and map context to evaluate VLMs' geo-temporal intelligence.
Contribution. Benchmark shows top VLMs like Gemini-2.5-Pro achieve only 34.9% accuracy versus 78.61% human performance, revealing three key deficiencies in spatial-temporal reasoning.
multi-perspective reasoninggeographic reasoningtemporal forecasting evaluation
ICLR 2026

SpatiaLab: Can Vision–Language Models Perform Spatial Reasoning in the Wild?

Problem. VLMs struggle with spatial reasoning in realistic, unconstrained contexts, yet prior work relied on synthetic or puzzle-like setups.
Approach. Introduces SpatiaLab with 1,400 QA pairs across six categories and 30 task types covering relative positioning, depth, orientation, size, navigation, and 3D geometry.
Contribution. Reveals substantial gaps in spatial reasoning: InternVL3.5-72B achieves 54.93% vs 87.57% human accuracy, showing key limitations in complex spatial understanding.
spatial reasoning benchmarkvision-language model evaluationreal-world spatial tasks
ICLR 2026

Unfolding Spatial Cognition: Evaluating Multimodal Models on Visual Simulations

Problem. Existing benchmarks assess verbal reasoning alone, not the multi-step visual simulation skills essential for spatial cognition.
Approach. STARE introduces a 3K-task benchmark spanning 2D/3D geometric transformations, cube net folding, tangram puzzles, and real-world spatial reasoning, evaluating multimodal LLMs on complex visual simulation.
Contribution. Evaluations reveal models perform near random chance on complex spatial tasks requiring multi-step visual simulation, while humans achieve near-perfect accuracy despite longer completion times.

PerceptionRubrics: Calibrating Multimodal Evaluation to Human Perception

Problem. Benchmark saturation creates dissonance between high benchmark scores and real-world brittleness in multimodal models.
Approach. PerceptionRubrics pairs 1,038 information-dense images with 12,000+ instance-specific rubrics derived from golden captions via Circular Peer-Review consensus, using Must-Right (essential facts) and Easy-Wrong (fine-grained details) categories with a Gated Scoring mechanism implementing sharp binary penalties.
Contribution. Reveals persistent 5% perception deficit between open-source and proprietary models, demonstrates fragmented element verification despite poor conjunctive constraint satisfaction, and achieves better human alignment than conventional benchmarks.

Papers evaluate whether language models actually think through problems logically, trace their reasoning faithfully, and catch when they make mistakes — rather than just guessing the right answer.

CogniLoad: A Synthetic Natural Language Reasoning Benchmark With Tunable Length, Intrinsic Difficulty, and Distractor Density

Problem. Current benchmarks for long-context reasoning blur critical factors like intrinsic task complexity, distractor interference, and task length, making failure analysis imprecise.
Approach. CogniLoad generates natural-language logic puzzles with independently tunable parameters reflecting Cognitive Load Theory: intrinsic difficulty d, distractor-to-signal ratio ρ, and task length N.
Contribution. Identifies task length as dominant constraint and reveals varied tolerances to intrinsic complexity and U-shaped responses to distractor ratios across 22 reasoning LLMs.

LINGOLY-TOO: Disentangling Reasoning from Knowledge with Templatised Orthographic Obfuscation

Problem. Frontier language models appear strong at reasoning but performance is often inflated by shortcuts such as memorisation and knowledge rather than genuine reasoning.
Approach. LingOLY-TOO applies expert-designed obfuscations to Linguistics Olympiad problems preserving solution logic while removing orthographic clues that trigger memorisation patterns.
Contribution. Model performance markedly drops upon obfuscation (best reasoning models drop from 0.60 to 0.48), effectively disentangling reasoning from knowledge.

Draw-In-Mind: Rebalancing Designer-Painter Roles in Unified Multimodal Models Benefits Image Editing

Problem. Unified multimodal models struggle with precise image editing because the understanding module acts merely as a translator while the generation module must simultaneously function as designer and painter, creating an imbalanced division of responsibilities.
Approach. Draw-In-Mind (DIM) rebalances roles by using DIM-T2I (14M image-text pairs) for complex instruction comprehension and DIM-Edit (233K chain-of-thought imaginations from GPT-4o) as explicit design blueprints for edits.
Contribution. DIM-4.6B-Edit achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance on ImgEdit and GEdit-Bench benchmarks, outperforming much larger models like UniWorld-V1 and Step1X-Edit.

Don’t Pass@$k$: A Bayesian Framework for Large Language Model Evaluation

Problem. Pass@k and average accuracy evaluations of LLM reasoning yield unstable and misleading model rankings, especially with limited samples and constrained compute.
Approach. A Bayesian framework models evaluation outcomes as categorical with Dirichlet priors, yielding posterior estimates of success probability and credible intervals for stable ranking.
Contribution. Faster convergence and greater rank stability than Pass@k on benchmark datasets (AIME, HMMT, BrUMO) with clear decision rules for statistical significance.

Efficient Bayesian Inference from Noisy Pairwise Comparisons

Problem. Aggregating noisy pairwise comparisons into quality scores requires careful modeling of rater variability, but existing Bradley-Terry methods either ignore rater quality or lack convergence guarantees.
Approach. Introduces BBQ, a Bayesian Bradley-Terry variant that explicitly models rater quality and provides monotonic likelihood convergence via Expectation-Maximization algorithm.
Contribution. Delivers efficient inference with well-calibrated uncertainty estimates and robust rankings even with noisy crowdsourced raters, enabling cost-effective human evaluation.

Building Better Benchmarks for New Capabilities 133

Papers design and construct new evaluation datasets for emerging abilities like agents solving real problems, code generation, scientific discovery, and handling specialized domains that weren't tested before.

Testing what agents can do in realistic environments

26

These papers create benchmarks to measure how well AI agents handle real-world tasks like writing code, using command lines, interacting with websites, and navigating game worlds—capturing the complexity of actual tool use.

Problem. Current evaluation benchmarks do not adequately assess LLM agents' ability to solve real-world tasks requiring synthesis of information from multiple sources and inferring insights.
Approach. DEEPSYNTH is a benchmark with 120 tasks across 7 domains and 42 countries designed using multi-stage data collection requiring annotators to collect official sources, create hypotheses, and perform manual analysis.
Contribution. Demonstrates that state-of-the-art LLMs and agents achieve maximum F1 of 8.97, revealing that current agents struggle with hallucinations and reasoning over large information spaces.

FinSearchComp: Towards a Realistic, Expert-Level Evaluation of Financial Search and Reasoning

Problem. Financial agents require complex multi-step searches over time-sensitive, domain-specific data; existing benchmarks lack realistic financial tasks that reflect analyst workflows and require deep expertise to construct.
Approach. FinSearchComp comprises three realistic tasks (Time-Sensitive Data Fetching, Simple Historical Lookup, Complex Historical Investigation) with 635 expert-annotated questions validated by 70 professional financial experts across global and Greater China markets.
Contribution. First open-source financial agent benchmark; Grok 4 approaches expert-level accuracy on global markets; web search and financial plugins significantly improve agent performance.
financial reasoningsearch benchmarksagent evaluationmulti-step task construction
ICLR 2026

RECODE: A Benchmark for Research Code DEvelopment with Interactive Human Feedback

Problem. LLMs struggle to generate correct and executable research code because existing benchmarks use one-shot settings that ignore the iterative, feedback-driven nature of realistic scientific development workflows.
Approach. RECODE introduces a benchmark with 102 tasks evaluated through multi-turn human feedback, structured instructions, and a five-level feedback hierarchy; ReCodeAgent integrates this feedback into iterative code generation.
Contribution. Experiments on GPT-5, Claude-Sonnet-4, DeepSeek-V3.1, and Gemini 2.5 show substantial performance gains with richer feedback, establishing a foundation for feedback-driven LLM agents in scientific research.
multi-turn interactionhuman feedback hierarchyiterative code generation

AgentSuite: Toward More Reliable Agent Evaluation with a Component-Based Benchmark Auditing Pipeline

Problem. Agent benchmarks contain hidden validity flaws from interactions among user instructions, environments, tools, and evaluation protocols that confound model errors with benchmark artifacts.

Contribution. Achieves F1 scores 0.791-0.874 aligning with expert judgments and identifies missed issues in tau-bench; released AgentSuite platform.

Problem. Standard evaluation metrics hide whether AI agents fail consistently, predictably, or with bounded severity when deployed in practice, despite benchmark improvements.

Contribution. Recent capability gains yield only small improvements in reliability across 15 models on two benchmarks, exposing persistent limitations beyond what single-metric evaluations reveal.

Outrunning LLM Cutoffs: A Live Kernel Crash Resolution Benchmark for All

Problem. Evaluating LLM agents for kernel crash resolution is complicated by static benchmarks with data contamination risk from LLM knowledge cutoffs.

ICML 2026

DRIFT-BENCH: Diagnosing CoopeRative Breakdowns in LLM Agents under Input Faults via Multi-Turn Interaction

Problem. As LLMs transition to autonomous agents, user inputs frequently violate cooperative assumptions (implicit intent, missing parameters, false presuppositions), creating execution risks not captured by text-only evaluations.
Approach. DRIFT-BENCH provides the first diagnostic benchmark evaluating agentic pragmatics through multi-turn clarification across state-oriented and service-oriented environments, grounded in communication theory with persona-driven user simulator.
Contribution. Experiments show substantial performance drops under input faults with varying clarification effectiveness across personas and fault types, connecting clarification studies with agent benchmarking.

Problem. Existing logical reasoning benchmarks focus on canonical puzzles, risking memorization of solution patterns and masking deficiencies in novel rule application and strategy adaptation.
Approach. HardcoreLogic introduces 5,000+ puzzles across 10 games with systematic transformations along three dimensions: Increased Complexity, Uncommon Elements, and Unsolvable Puzzles.
Contribution. Reveals significant performance drops in Large Reasoning Models achieving top scores on existing benchmarks; exposes heavy reliance on memorized patterns and gaps in genuine reasoning.

Contribution. Reveals that GPT-5 achieves only 38.2% accuracy with web alone, domain tools provide selective benefits, and 22.4% of failures stem from incorrect tool usage.

Approach. FigEdit introduces a benchmark of 30,000+ samples covering 10 chart types with five progressively challenging tasks (single/multi/conversational/visual-guided edits and style transfer), exposing the limitations of pixel-level approaches.
Contribution. Evaluation of state-of-the-art models reveals poor performance on scientific figures and that traditional metrics like SSIM and PSNR have limitations in capturing semantic correctness of edits.

Problem. Game benchmarks lack comprehensive evaluation of LLM capabilities across genres and lack fine-tuning datasets to adapt pre-trained LLMs for gaming agents.
Approach. Orak provides a plug-and-play benchmark spanning 12 games across genres, with systematic evaluation of agentic modules and a fine-tuning dataset of expert gameplay trajectories to adapt LLMs into game agents.
Contribution. Establishes a comprehensive evaluation framework with leaderboards, battle arenas, and in-depth analysis of input modality and fine-tuning effects for versatile gaming agents.

Building benchmarks that evolve and stay challenging

44

These papers develop new ways to construct benchmarks dynamically—updating them as models improve, making them harder over time, or generating test cases automatically to prevent overfitting.

Gaia2: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Dynamic and Asynchronous Environments

Problem. Existing agent benchmarks are static or synchronous, failing to capture realistic scenarios where environments evolve independently and agents face temporal constraints, noise, and ambiguity.
Approach. Gaia2 introduces asynchronous environments where scenarios evolve independently of agent actions, paired with write-action verifiers for fine-grained evaluation, and built on the open-source Agents Research Environments platform.
Contribution. Gaia2 evaluation reveals trade-offs between reasoning, efficiency, and robustness across state-of-the-art models: GPT-5 reaches 42% pass@1 on overall performance but fails on time-sensitive tasks, while Kimi-K2 leads open-source at 21%.

OptimalThinkingBench: Evaluating Over and Underthinking in LLMs

Problem. Thinking LLMs overthink on simple problems while non-thinking LLMs underthink on hard reasoning, creating separate model variants and leaving end-users to select the optimal model.
Approach. Introduces a unified benchmark with OverthinkingBench (72 domains of simple queries) and UnderthinkingBench (11 challenging reasoning tasks) using thinking-adjusted accuracy metrics to jointly evaluate both phenomena.
Contribution. Shows that no model optimally thinks on the benchmark; thinking models often waste hundreds of tokens on simplest queries without improvement, highlighting the need for better unified models.

How Many Code and Test Cases Are Enough? Evaluating Test Cases Generation from a Binary-Matrix Perspective

Problem. Evaluating generated test case quality requires expensive scoring on large unstructured code collections, leading to high computational costs and benchmark inflation favoring trivial bug detection.
Approach. Formulates benchmark construction as finding an optimal diagnostic basis in a binary code-test matrix, where rank specifies minimum error patterns and test cases needed.
Contribution. Introduces novel framework using matrix rank for efficient benchmark construction that maximizes internal diversity and identifies minimal sufficient wrong codes and test cases.

Problem. Coding benchmarks evaluate LMs on isolated tasks, but real software development pursues high-level open-ended objectives iteratively without explicit guidance.
Approach. CodeClash is a multi-round tournament benchmark where LMs iteratively develop code to win objectives like score maximization or survival.
Contribution. Reveals diverse development styles and fundamental limitations in strategic reasoning; top models lose every round to expert humans.
benchmark designgoal-oriented developmentcompetitive evaluation
ICML 2026

iWorld-Bench: A Benchmark for Interactive World Models with a Unified Action Generation Framework

Problem. Existing world models lack large-scale datasets and unified benchmarks to evaluate interaction capabilities like distance perception and memory.
Approach. iWorld-Bench constructs diverse dataset with 330k clips, introduces Action Generation Framework unifying evaluation across six task types.
Contribution. Evaluates 14 world models identifying key limitations, provides public leaderboard and insights for future research.
world modelsbenchmark designmulti-task evaluation
ICML 2026

ASyMOB: Algebraic Symbolic Mathematical Operations Benchmark

Problem. Existing symbolic math benchmarks for LLMs conflate pattern memorization with genuine reasoning and lack fine-grained assessment of generalization.
Approach. Create ASyMOB with 35,368 symbolic math problems spanning integration, limits, differential equations, series, and hypergeometrics, systematically perturbing each seed problem via symbolic, numeric, and equivalence-preserving transformations.
Contribution. Reveal that most models' performance collapses under minor perturbations while top systems show regime shift in robustness, and identify cases where LLMs succeed while CAS fails.
benchmark constructionperturbation analysissymbolic mathematics
ICML 2026

Strategic Navigation or Stochastic Search? How Agents and Humans Reason Over Document Collections

Problem. Multimodal agents may succeed through brute-force search rather than genuine strategic reasoning when automating document-intensive workflows.
Approach. MADQA benchmark of 2250 questions over 800 PDFs and a novel accuracy-effort trade-off protocol measure agentic behavior, revealing brute-force search patterns.
Contribution. The benchmark shows best agents match human accuracy but on different questions, fail to close a 20% gap to oracle performance, and persist in unproductive loops.
benchmark designaccuracy-effort trade-offclassical test theory
ICML 2026

Leaderboard Incentives: Model Rankings under Strategic Post-Training

Problem. Benchmark design induces misaligned incentives for model builders, who strategically allocate resources to inflate benchmark performance rather than improve latent model quality.
Approach. Frames benchmarking as a Stackelberg game where benchmark designers choose evaluation protocols and model providers compete simultaneously; proves current protocols lack Nash equilibrium but tune-before-test induces unique equilibrium.
Contribution. Demonstrates benchmarks need not induce bad incentives and provides principled evaluation protocols that rank models by latent quality.
game theoryNash equilibriumtune-before-test protocol
ICML 2026

OPT-Engine: Benchmarking the Limits of LLMs in Optimization Modeling via Complexity Scaling

Problem. LLMs struggle with optimization modeling which requires structured reasoning and precise constraint formulation, with Pure-Text Reasoning suffering from critical robustness gaps and external tools failing to enforce global constraints.
Approach. OPT-Engine is an extensible benchmark with quantifiable complexity spanning ten canonical OR problems from Linear to Mixed-Integer Programming; tests three paradigms: Pure-Text Reasoning, tool-integrated reasoning, and Solver-integrated Reasoning.
Contribution. Identifies constraint formulation as the primary bottleneck in current LLM approaches and provides structured roadmap for developing next-generation LLMs for optimization modeling.
large language modelsconstraint formulationbenchmark designprompt engineering
ICML 2026

GENEB: Why Genomic Models Are Hard to Compare

Problem. Progress in genomic foundation models is difficult to assess due to fragmented benchmarks, incompatible evaluation protocols, and task-specific reporting making comparisons unreliable.
Approach. Introduces GENEB, a large-scale diagnostic benchmark evaluating frozen representations from 40 genomic models across 100 tasks spanning 13 categories under unified probing-based protocol.
Contribution. Reveals aggregate leaderboards are unstable with model rankings varying sharply across categories; scale provides modest inconsistent gains; architectural and pretraining alignment often outweigh parameter count.
genomic modelsbenchmark designprobingevaluation protocols

Judging quality when answers are subjective

13

These benchmarks tackle the hard problem of evaluating outputs that don't have single right answers—like story generation, creative writing, or summarization—often using other models or humans as judges.

ICLR 2026

VERINA: Benchmarking Verifiable Code Generation

Problem. Current benchmarks for verifiable code generation focus on individual components rather than providing holistic evaluation of code, specification, and proof generation together.
Approach. VERINA is a high-quality benchmark with 189 manually curated Lean coding tasks including problem descriptions, reference implementations, formal specifications, and test suites enabling comprehensive modular evaluation.
Contribution. Reveals significant challenges in verifiable code generation, with the best model (OpenAI o3) achieving 72.6% code correctness, 52.3% specification soundness, and only 4.9% proof success rate.
code generationformal verificationtheorem provingbenchmarking

Problem. Reference-based reward systems for reasoning model training lack standardized evaluation benchmarks; existing benchmarks focus on preference comparisons rather than verification.
Approach. VerifyBench provides carefully curated and annotated benchmarks specifically designed to assess reference-based reward systems through meticulous data collection.
Contribution. Establishes standardized framework for evaluating verification systems; reveals all current systems have substantial room for improvement on challenging instances.
reference-based reward evaluationbenchmark constructionverification systems

Sci2Pol: Evaluating and Fine-tuning LLMs on Scientific-to-Policy Brief Generation

Problem. Large language models lack task-specific training and benchmarks for translating scientific papers into policy briefs, a distinct generation task that existing metrics like ROUGE fail to evaluate properly.
Approach. We create Sci2Pol-Bench with 18 tasks mirroring the human writing process (autocompletion, understanding, summarization, generation, verification) and introduce an LLM-based metric aligned with expert judgment.
Contribution. After fine-tuning on our curated 639-example corpus, Gemma-27B surpasses GPT-4o and DeepSeek-V3 on the benchmark.

Problem. Evaluating LLM-based agentic systems acting as co-scientists requires realistic end-to-end research scenarios integrating data analysis, interpretation, and new insights from experimental data.
Approach. HeurekaBench creates benchmarks with exploratory, open-ended research questions grounded in scientific studies; uses a semi-automated pipeline with multiple LLMs to extract insights and verify against reported findings.

StoryAlign: Evaluating and Training Reward Models for Story Generation

Problem. Story generation by LLMs diverges from human-authored works regarding complex narrative structure and human-aligned preferences due to absence of effective modeling of subjective human story preferences.

VeriEquivBench: An Equivalence Score for Ground-Truth-Free Evaluation of Formally Verifiable Code

Problem. Formal verification of LLM-generated code is bottlenecked by manual ground-truth specification matching, limiting datasets to hundreds of problems and raising reliability concerns.
Approach. VeriEquivBench replaces ground-truth matching with equivalence score—a formally grounded metric—rigorously verifying generated specifications and code quality without manual annotation.
Contribution. VeriEquivBench with 2,389 complex algorithmic problems reveals that formally verifiable code generation remains profoundly challenging for state-of-the-art LLMs.

CoDA: From Text-to-Image Diffusion Models to Training-Free Dataset Distillation

Problem. Dataset distillation with diffusion models requires target-specific pre-training, creating circular dependency, and general text-to-image models suffer from distributional mismatch.
Approach. CoDA identifies the intrinsic core distribution of the target dataset and steers text-to-image generation to align with it via density-based discovery mechanism.
Contribution. Achieves performance on par with or better than target-specific distillation methods across ImageNet-1K and subsets without requiring target-specific training.
dataset distillationcore distribution alignmentdensity discovery

Approach. UFEval learns joint assessment of multiple visual criteria and tasks, leveraging FRABench dataset with hierarchical taxonomy of 112 aspects across four evaluation tasks.
Contribution. First unified fine-grained evaluator with task and aspect generalization achieving 60.4k pairwise samples with 325k labels for multimodal evaluation.

Problem. Assessing trustworthiness of generative foundation models is fragmented, rapidly outdated, and lacks extensibility across modalities, with no systematic and continuous evaluation approach.

Contribution. Evaluating 39 models reveals that state-of-the-art GenFMs achieve promising overall trust but have significant limitations in hallucination resistance, fairness, and privacy preservation.

Detecting When Models Are Wrong or Biased 66

Papers measure model uncertainty, robustness to tricky inputs, and hidden biases — catching cases where models confidently give wrong answers or fail on edge cases.

ICLR 2026

Semantic Visual Anomaly Detection and Reasoning in AI-Generated Images

Problem. AI-generated images often exhibit subtle semantic anomalies like unrealistic object configurations, physical law violations, or commonsense inconsistencies that compromise plausibility.
Approach. We introduce AnomReason, a large-scale benchmark with structured annotations (Name, Phenomenon, Reasoning, Severity) produced by a modular multi-agent pipeline (AnomAgent) with lightweight human verification.
Contribution. Models fine-tuned on AnomReason achieve consistent gains on semantic matching metrics, with applications to explainable deepfake detection and semantic reasonableness assessment.

FATE: A Formal Benchmark Series for Frontier Algebra of Multiple Difficulty Levels

Problem. Contest-based math benchmarks like the IMO do not reflect the depth, breadth, and abstraction of modern mathematical research, limiting evaluation of formal reasoning.
Approach. FATE introduces a benchmark series (FATE-H and FATE-X) with 100 problems each in abstract and commutative algebra spanning from undergraduate to PhD-level difficulty, with systematic evaluation of formalization errors.
Contribution. FATE-X is the first formal benchmark surpassing both PhD-level exam difficulty and Mathlib library coverage; best models achieve 3% on FATE-H and 0% on FATE-X, revealing a stark performance gap.

Analyzing and Evaluating Unbiased Language Model Watermark

Problem. Unbiased watermarking methods for LLM outputs lack standardized evaluation protocols and can accumulate distributional bias over multiple generations.
Approach. UWBench provides a principled evaluation framework with theoretical analysis including impossibility results, statistical metrics for distribution shift, and a three-axis evaluation protocol (unbiasedness, detectability, robustness).
Contribution. UWBench reveals that no unbiased watermark can perfectly preserve distribution under infinite queries and offers standardized benchmarking for advancing watermarking design.

Problem. Conformal prediction methods with localization produce prediction sets with high variability in size, especially with limited calibration data.
Approach. Stable Conformal Prediction (StCP) uses transfer learning from labeled source-task data and unlabeled target data to improve set stability without additional target labels.
Contribution. Delivers more stable prediction sets than standard conformal methods, especially for localized approaches when calibration data are limited.
conformal predictiontransfer learninguncertainty quantification
ICML 2026

Conf-Gen: Conformal Uncertainty Quantification for Generative Models

Problem. Conformal prediction and risk control frameworks are incompatible with unsupervised generative models like LLMs and image generators.
Approach. Conformal Generation extends conformal risk control to generative tasks by relaxing theoretical assumptions, unifying and generalizing previous CP applications to LLMs.
Contribution. Enable formal guarantees on image generators producing non-memorized images, conversational AI asking enough clarifying questions, and agent output correctness.
conformal predictiongenerative modelsuncertainty quantification
ICML 2026

VocSim A Training-free Benchmark for Zero-shot Content Identity in Single-source Audio

Problem. Evaluating general-purpose audio representations on content identity tasks requires methods that measure intrinsic geometric alignment without parameter updates or labels.
Approach. VocSim is a training-free benchmark aggregating 125k single-source clips from 19 corpora (speech, animal vocalizations, environmental sounds) with label-free PCA whitening and evaluation metrics including Precision@k and Global Separation Rate.
Contribution. Frozen Whisper features with simple pooling and PCA achieve strong zero-shot performance with stable rankings across domains, revealing a cross-lingual speech generalization gap on low-resource languages.
zero-shot representation learningcontent identity benchmarkingcross-lingual evaluation
ICML 2026

Conformal Calibration Transfer

Problem. Conformal prediction fails when labeled calibration is only available in a source domain while prediction sets are needed in a target domain without labeled calibration.
Approach. TCC transports source calibration into the target space using paired data, then corrects residual mismatch using label-free uncertainty surrogates with conservative adjustment or reweighting.
Contribution. The method provides finite-sample target-domain coverage guarantees that adapt to observable mismatch magnitude without labeled target calibration.
conformal predictiontransport learningdomain adaptation
ICML 2026

MMBench-Live: A Continuously Evolving Benchmark for Multimodal Models

Problem. Most multimodal benchmarks are static, vulnerable to temporal staleness, data contamination, and costly maintenance, hindering sustainable evaluation of vision-language models.
Approach. MMBench-Live uses multi-agent-driven automated pipeline with structured specification, feedback-controlled real-time acquisition, and verifiable QA generation; maintains cross-version comparability via distribution-consistent updates.
Contribution. Contains 5.9K newly generated instances with high answer correctness, updates cost ~$30 and take 1-2 hours, preserves stable model rankings and exhibits weaker memorization.
automated pipelinedistribution-consistent updatesexecutable reasoning verification
ICML 2026

Questioning the Coverage-Length Metric in Conformal Prediction: When Shorter Intervals Are Not Better

Problem. Standard conformal prediction metrics (coverage and interval length) can be deceptively manipulated through techniques that preserve coverage while artificially reducing interval length, creating instability across repeated inference runs.
Approach. The work identifies and formalizes the 'Prejudicial Trick' technique that creates misleading improvements while preserving marginal coverage, then introduces interval stability as a new metric to detect such PT-like manipulations.
Contribution. Formally derives conditions under which interval length improvements are deceptive and provides empirical evidence across regression and classification tasks.
conformal predictioninterval stabilitycoverage analysis
ICML 2026

Nonparametric LLM Evaluation from Preference Data

Problem. Evaluating LLMs from human preference data using parametric approaches lacks flexibility or requires black-box methods without valid uncertainty quantification.
Approach. DMLRank proposes nonparametric framework using debiased machine learning to estimate Generalized Average Ranking Scores generalizing Bradley-Terry and PageRank with support for ties.
Contribution. Produces statistically efficient estimates, incorporates black-box ML and pre-trained LLM evaluators, suggests optimal preference data collection policies.
debiased machine learningranking modelsuncertainty quantification
ICML 2026

R1-SyntheticVL: Is Synthetic Data from Generative Models Ready for Multimodal Large Language Model?

Problem. Synthesizing high-quality, diverse multimodal training data autonomously for enhancing MLLMs is challenging without sacrificing either quality or challenge level.
Approach. CADS operates through Collective Adversarial Data Generation (CAD-Generate) leveraging collective knowledge for diverse generation, and Collective Adversarial Data Judgment (CAD-Judge) for quality assessment, with Adversarial Context Optimization to encourage challenging samples.
Contribution. R1-SyntheticVL trained on synthesized MMSynthetic-20K demonstrates superior performance on various benchmarks through adversarial synthesis.
synthetic data generationadversarial learningquality judgment
ICML 2026

Improving Backward Conformal Prediction via Non-Conformity Score Transformation

Problem. Backward Conformal Prediction achieves predefined set-size bounds but Markov's inequality causes significant gaps between estimated coverage bounds and empirical coverage.
Approach. ST-BCP introduces a data-dependent transformation of nonconformity scores to narrow the coverage gap; develops a computable transformation proven to outperform baseline identity transformation.
Contribution. Reduces average coverage gap from 4.20% to 1.12% on common benchmarks.
conformal predictionuncertainty quantificationscore transformation
ICML 2026

TokSuite: Measuring the Impact of Tokenizer Choice on Language Model Behavior

Problem. Tokenizer role in LM performance and behavior is poorly understood due to difficulty measuring tokenization effects in isolation.
Approach. TokSuite releases fourteen pre-trained models with different tokenizers but identical architecture, dataset, and training budget, plus multilingual robustness benchmark.
Contribution. Enables robust decoupling of tokenizer influence with findings elucidating benefits and shortcomings of popular tokenizers across languages.
tokenization analysiscontrolled comparisonrobustness evaluation
ICML 2026

Conditional Coverage Diagnostics for Conformal Prediction

Problem. Evaluating conditional coverage of conformal prediction sets is challenging; existing metrics suffer from sample inefficiency, overfitting, and lack power to detect coverage violations.
Approach. Cast conditional coverage estimation as a classification problem where violations occur iff some classifier achieves lower risk than target; derive excess risk of target coverage (ERT) via proper loss functions.
Contribution. ERT provides conservative estimates of coverage miscoverage measures with higher statistical power than established metrics like CovGap, with an open-source implementation and benchmark across conformal methods.
conformal predictioncoverage evaluationclassification-based testing

Papers improve how benchmarks are scored and designed so results are reproducible, reliable, and reveal genuine progress rather than just progress on specific test data.

Problem. Urban LLM agents lack proper evaluation of their spatiotemporal reasoning abilities, with limited insight into underlying reasoning processes and unclear performance on long-horizon tasks.
Approach. USTBench introduces a benchmark with 62,466 structured QA pairs evaluating four decomposed dimensions (understanding, forecasting, planning, reflection) within an interactive city environment across 9 tasks.

Benchmarking Stochastic Approximation Algorithms for Fairness-Constrained Training of Deep Neural Networks

Problem. Training deep neural networks with fairness constraints lacks standardized, widely accepted methods and comprehensive benchmarks for evaluation.
Approach. Provides challenging benchmark on real-world large-scale fairness-constrained tasks using US Census data; reviews stochastic approximation algorithms and implements three recently proposed methods.
Contribution. Establishes benchmark for comparing fairness-constrained learning algorithms in terms of optimization performance and fairness improvement.
fairness-constrained trainingstochastic approximationbenchmark evaluation
ICLR 2026

Choices Speak Louder than Questions

Problem. Multiple-choice question answering evaluation may not accurately reflect LLM comprehension, as models can be influenced by answer options more than genuine understanding of questions.
Approach. Normalized Probability Shift by the Question (NPSQ) scoring method isolates the impact of the question itself, remaining stable even when answer options are modified.
Contribution. NPSQ demonstrates stability across various input formats (cloze, symbols, hybrid) while traditional scoring methods remain vulnerable to superficial characteristics of answer choices.
mcqa evaluationscoring methodscomprehension assessment

Contribution. First cryptocurrency benchmark systematizing 452-token dataset evaluation across eight TSG methods, revealing trade-offs between statistical quality and trading profitability.

SAIR: Enabling Deep Learning for Protein-Ligand Interactions with a Synthetic Structural Dataset

ICML 2026

BRIDGE: Predicting Human Task Completion Time From Model Performance

Problem. Grounding benchmark performance in human task completion time requires costly, noisy annotations that don't scale; pre-hoc prediction of this metric remains unexplored.
Approach. BRIDGE uses Item Response Theory to jointly estimate latent task difficulty and model capability from performance data; aligns difficulty linearly with log(human time).
Contribution. Infers human task completion time for new benchmarks from model performance; reproduces METR's exponential scaling with 50% horizon doubling every 6 months.

ICML 2026

Diagnosing the Reliability of LLM-as-a-Judge via Item Response Theory

Problem. LLM-as-a-Judge is widely used in automated evaluation, but existing validation practices offer limited insight into whether LLM judges themselves function as stable and reliable measurement instruments.
Approach. A two-phase diagnostic framework grounded in Item Response Theory (IRT) adopts the Graded Response Model to assess reliability along intrinsic consistency (stability under prompt variations) and human alignment dimensions.
Contribution. Leveraging IRT-GRM yields interpretable signals for diagnosing LLM judge reliability systematically and identifying potential causes of unreliability.
item response theorygraded response modelreliability diagnostics
ICML 2026

InteractComp: Evaluating Search Agents With Ambiguous Queries

Problem. Search agent benchmarks assume complete unambiguous queries, leaving under-tested practical failure: agents lack interactive mechanisms to resolve ambiguous requests during search.
Approach. InteractComp benchmarks 210 expert-curated questions with controlled ambiguity, evaluating if agents recognize ambiguity and actively interact to resolve it during search.
Contribution. Reveals striking failure with best model achieving only 13.73% despite 71.50% with complete context, with forced interaction producing dramatic gains and demonstrating latent capability.

ICML 2026

Adaptive Testing for LLM Evaluation: A Psychometric Alternative to Static Benchmarks

Problem. Evaluating LLMs with static benchmarks requires thousands of items and treats all items equally despite vast differences in difficulty and informativeness.
Approach. ATLAS applies Item Response Theory with Fisher information-guided item selection to adaptively choose the most informative items, reducing evaluation items needed while maintaining precision.
Contribution. ATLAS reduces required items by up to 90% while maintaining measurement precision and detecting meaningful ability differences among models with identical accuracies.
item response theoryfisher informationadaptive testing
ICML 2026

Item Response Scaling Laws: A Measurement Theory Approach for Efficient and Generalizable Neural Scaling Estimation

Problem. Scaling law derivation requires expensive evaluations across thousands of checkpoints and millions of samples, making practical LLM analysis prohibitively costly.
Approach. IRSL integrates Item Response Theory into scaling laws, disentangling model ability from question characteristics to reduce complexity from O(M×N) to O(M+N).
Contribution. Achieves reliable scaling estimates with only 50 questions per benchmark (99.9% reduction) after one-time calibration, with generalizable model ability estimates.

Beyond Benchmarks: Toward Causally Faithful Evaluation of Large Language Models

Problem. LLM evaluations conflate benchmark performance with intrinsic capability, overlooking that observed outcomes arise from the entire evaluation system and that evaluation choices alone can induce 70% accuracy swings.

Contribution. The framework enables more interpretable, reproducible, and carefully attributed assessment of model capability by probing dataset-induced effects and jointly modeling evaluation components.

When AI Benchmarks Plateau: A Systematic Study of Benchmark Saturation

Problem. Many AI benchmarks quickly saturate, losing ability to differentiate between top-performing models and diminishing evaluation value.
Approach. Analyzes 60 LLM benchmarks across 14 properties (task design, data construction, evaluation format) and tests five hypotheses on saturation drivers using empirical characterization.
Contribution. Finds nearly half of benchmarks exhibit saturation, increasing with age; expert-curated benchmarks resist saturation better than crowdsourced ones, while data privacy shows no protective effect.
