Problem. Mixup data augmentation's effectiveness on graph data is uncertain, with no rigorous independent empirical evidence for state-of-the-art graph mixup methods despite widespread adoption in vision and language domains.
Approach. Extensive evaluation of graph mixup methods following a unified protocol for graph classification, analyzing both interpolation properties and downstream task performance.
Contribution. Finds that state-of-the-art mixup methods yield no statistically significant improvements over no-mixup baselines and that high interpolation error leads to performance degradation.
data augmentationgraph mixupempirical evaluation
ICML 2026

Training Data Efficiency in Multimodal Process Reward Models

Problem. Training multimodal process reward models requires large-scale annotated corpora with substantial redundancy, making data efficiency critical.
Approach. Formalizes theoretical framework showing informative updates depend on label mixtures and reliability, then proposes Balanced-Information Score prioritizing both factors based on rollout-level MC signals.
Contribution. BIS-selected subsets reach full-data performance using only 10% of training data on InternVL2.5-8B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B, improving over random subsampling by 4.1% relative.
data-efficient trainingbalanced selectionMC-based scoring
ICML 2026

DIVER: Diving Deeper into Distilled Data via Expressive Semantic Recovery

Problem. Single-stage dataset distillation overfits to a prior architecture and suppresses semantic expression, leading to poor cross-architecture generalization and privacy-utility degradation.
Approach. DIVER leverages a pre-trained diffusion model in a dual-stage framework with semantic inheritance to filter architecture-specific noise, semantic guidance to improve original semantics, and semantic fusion to prevent ambiguity during reverse diffusion.
Contribution. DIVER significantly improves cross-architecture generalization of distilled data while requiring processing time comparable to raw DiT on ImageNet (256×256) with only 4 GB GPU memory.
dataset distillationdiffusion modelssemantic preservation
ICML 2026

Memory-Distilled Selection for Noise-Robust Anomaly Detection

Problem. Anomaly detection in industrial settings requires robustness to data contamination, but existing methods degrade significantly as noise ratio increases without noise-specific tuning.
Approach. Memory-Distilled Selection (MeDS) constructs an ensemble of partial memories via random subsampling where sparsity filters nominal patterns, then distills into a reconstruction score network.
Contribution. Achieves 99.16% image-level AUROC on MVTecAD at 40% noise ratio and state-of-the-art on VisA and Real-IAD under noisy conditions without hyperparameter tuning.
anomaly detectionensemble methodsdata selectionnoise robustness
ICML 2026

Scalable RF Simulation in Generative 4D Worlds

Problem. Building high-quality RF datasets in dynamic and diverse environments for privacy-preserving RF sensing remains a major challenge despite RF's advantages over vision methods.
Approach. WaveVerse is a prompt-based framework simulating realistic RF signals from generated indoor scenes with human motions, using a language-guided 4D world generator and phase-coherent ray tracer.
Contribution. Simulated signals show high fidelity on phase-sensitive benchmarks and improve downstream RF imaging and activity recognition tasks with gains that grow with data volume.
RF simulation4D scene generationray tracingdata augmentation
ICML 2026

Relational In-Context Learning via Synthetic Pre-training with Structural Prior

Problem. Relational databases lack foundation models comparable to those in text or vision due to data scarcity and structural heterogeneity; internet-scale pretraining is infeasible.
Approach. RDB-PFN, inspired by Prior-Data Fitted Networks, uses a Relational Prior Generator to create infinite synthetic diverse RDBs from structural causal models, pretraining purely on synthetic relational tasks.
Contribution. RDB-PFN achieves strong few-shot performance on 19 real-world relational prediction tasks, outperforming state-of-the-art tabular foundation models with lightweight architecture and fast inference.
synthetic data generationprior-data fitted networksstructural causal modelsin-context learning
ICML 2026

PluRel: Synthetic Data unlocks Scaling Laws for Relational Foundation Models

Problem. Diverse relational databases needed to train Relational Foundation Models are rarely public due to privacy constraints, and existing methods fail to synthesize multi-table data with schema structure.
Approach. PluRel framework synthesizes multi-tabular databases by modeling schemas with directed graphs, inter-table connectivity with bipartite graphs, and feature distributions via conditional causal mechanisms.
Contribution. First demonstration that RFM pretraining loss exhibits power-law scaling with synthetic databases; synthetic pretraining provides strong base for real database fine-tuning.
synthetic data generationrelational databasesscaling laws
ICML 2026

Is Data Shapley Not Better than Random in Data Selection? Ask NASH

Problem. Data Shapley-based selection sometimes performs no better than random selection, raising questions about when Shapley-based approaches work well and how to reliably identify high-quality data subsets.
Approach. Decompose the utility function into simpler Shapley-informative components and aggregate them non-linearly via a framework called NASH that strategically leverages settings where Shapley values are most informative.
Contribution. NASH substantially improves the effectiveness of Shapley-based data selection with minimal computational overhead by identifying and exploiting Shapley-informative components.
data shapleyvalue decompositionnon-linear aggregation

Testing Specialized Reasoning and Knowledge 13

Papers create benchmarks for narrow domains like medical diagnosis, chemistry, law, theorem proving, or biological science — where background knowledge and careful reasoning both matter.

ICLR 2026

Can You Hear Me Now? A Benchmark for Long-Range Graph Propagation

Problem. Graph Neural Networks struggle to capture long-range interactions due to information bottlenecks, a critical challenge for applications requiring distant node communication.
Approach. ECHO is a benchmark with three synthetic tasks (shortest paths, node eccentricity, graph diameter) on challenging topologies, plus two real-world datasets (ECHO-Charge, ECHO-Energy) with DFT-level references for molecular properties.
Contribution. Benchmarking reveals clear performance gaps in long-range propagation, establishing ECHO as a new standard for evaluating long-range information propagation in GNNs.
graph neural network benchmarkingsynthetic propagation tasksmolecular property prediction
ICLR 2026

3DCS: Datasets and Benchmark for Evaluating Conformational Sensitivity in Molecular Representations

Problem. Despite rapid development of molecular representation models, there is no comprehensive benchmark to evaluate how well they capture 3D conformational information critical for reaction prediction and drug design.
Approach. Introduces 3DCS, a benchmark with three large-scale datasets (>1M molecules, ~10M conformers) and a Geometry-Chirality-Energy (GCE) evaluation framework to assess conformational sensitivity.
Contribution. Reveals that modern data-driven representations handle geometry well but inconsistently handle chirality and poorly align with energy landscapes, providing the first rigorous benchmark for 3D molecular representations.
molecular representation benchmarkingconformational analysisenergy landscape evaluation

MolLangBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Language-Prompted Molecular Structure Recognition, Editing, and Generation

Problem. Current AI systems struggle with precise molecular recognition, editing, and generation even for intuitively simple chemistry tasks.
Approach. MolLangBench evaluates fundamental molecule-language interface tasks using automated cheminformatics for recognition, expert annotation for editing/generation, supporting linear strings, molecular images, and graphs.
Contribution. Shows strongest model (GPT-5) achieves only 86.2% and 85.5% on recognition and editing, and 43.0% on generation, revealing significant limitations in molecular manipulation.

Contribution. Reveals GPT-5-high achieves only 65.9% overall performance on professional-domain tasks and identifies notable disparities between proprietary and open-weight models.

Problem. Evaluating true inductive generalization of LLMs on entirely unseen data is increasingly challenging as reasoning capabilities expand.

Problem. Benchmarking hundreds of functional connectivity modeling methods on large-scale fMRI datasets is computationally prohibitive due to combinatorial explosion of model-data pairings.
Approach. SCLCS proposes structure-aware contrastive learning framework selecting small representative core-sets that preserve relative performance ranking of FC operators, using adaptive Transformer and novel Structural Perturbation Score.
Contribution. Preserves ground-truth model ranking with just 10% of data, outperforming state-of-the-art core-set selection by up to 23.2% in ranking consistency.

SparseEval: Efficient Evaluation of Large Language Models by Sparse Optimization

Problem. Evaluating large language models on benchmarks is expensive due to high computational costs of inference on large numbers of samples.
Approach. SparseEval formulates efficient benchmarking as a sparse optimization problem by identifying sparsity in the model-item performance matrix, selecting representative anchor items, and using gradient descent to optimize anchor weights with iterative refinement.
Contribution. SparseEval demonstrates low estimation error and high Kendall's tau across benchmarks, showcasing superior robustness and practicality for efficient LLM evaluation in real-world scenarios.

Problem. Existing benchmarks for LLM logical consistency are not scalable or challenging, with state-of-the-art models already surpassing 95% accuracy on simple tasks.
Approach. LogiConBench automatically generates unlimited logical rule combinations with precise labels and controllable-depth reasoning graphs, translating symbolic logic into diverse natural language.
Contribution. Provides 280K scalable samples remaining challenging for frontier LLMs; the Enumerative task achieves only 34% exact accuracy for the best model, establishing a robust benchmark.
logical reasoningsynthetic benchmarksconsistency checkinggraph generation

GIQ: Benchmarking 3D Geometric Reasoning of Vision Foundation Models with Simulated and Real Polyhedra

Problem. Monocular 3D reconstruction methods and vision-language models demonstrate impressive results on standard benchmarks, but their true understanding of geometric properties remains unclear.
Approach. Develop comprehensive benchmark with synthetic and real-world images of diverse polyhedra; evaluate through 3D reconstruction, symmetry detection, mental rotation, and shape classification tasks.
Contribution. Reveals significant shortcomings: SOTA algorithms struggle reconstructing basic geometric forms; VLMs falter on tasks requiring detailed geometric differentiation.

Agent-X: Evaluating Deep Multimodal Reasoning in Vision-Centric Agentic Tasks

Problem. Existing vision-centric agent benchmarks use fully synthetic single-turn queries with limited visual modalities, lacking evaluation of multi-step reasoning in real-world settings.
Approach. Introduces Agent-X, a benchmark with 828 authentic agentic tasks across six environments (visual reasoning, web browsing, security, autonomous driving, sports, math), with step-level evaluation framework.
Contribution. Reveals even best-performing models (GPT, Gemini, Qwen families) achieve less than 50% full-chain success on multi-step vision tasks, identifying key reasoning and tool-use bottlenecks.

Credit-Budgeted ICPC-Style Coding: When LLM Agents Must Pay for Every Decision

Problem. Coding-agent benchmarks ignore real-world resource constraints where every LLM call, test, and rollback incurs deterministic costs.
Approach. USACOArena is ICPC-inspired arena with credit-based cost system where agents pay for prompts, compilations, tests, and rollbacks, revealing decision profiles and policy spaces.
Contribution. Exposes hidden costs and ROI of different agent strategies, demonstrating rich policy space where identical models diverge in self-play depending on risk/budget trade-offs.

How Reliable is Language Model Micro-Benchmarking?

Problem. Micro-benchmarking claims to offer efficient model evaluation, but it's unclear whether small subsets can rank models as reliably as full benchmarks and whether they outperform random sampling.
Approach. A meta-evaluation measure investigates how well micro-benchmarks rank model pairs as a function of performance difference on full benchmarks, enabling finer-grained analysis of the size-reliability trade-off.
Contribution. The work shows that no micro-benchmarking method can consistently rank models 3.5 points apart on MMLU-Pro or 4 points on BIG-Bench Hard, requiring up to 250 examples for reliable comparisons.
meta-evaluationmicro-benchmark reliability analysismodel ranking consistency

MCP Security Bench (MSB): Benchmarking Attacks Against Model Context Protocol in LLM Agents

Problem. Model Context Protocol (MCP) for LLM agents standardizes tool access but enlarges the attack surface with little systematic evaluation of security.
Approach. MSB introduces a taxonomy of 12 MCP-specific attacks (name-collision, prompt injection, parameter manipulation, etc.) and an evaluation harness executing real benign and malicious tools via MCP across 10 domains.
Contribution. MSB evaluates 9 LLM agents across 405 tools with 2,000 attack instances, revealing vulnerability patterns and introducing Net Resilient Performance (NRP) to quantify the security-performance tradeoff.

PluriHarms: Benchmarking the Full Spectrum of Human Judgments on AI Harm

Problem. Current AI safety frameworks treat harmfulness as binary and lack flexibility to handle borderline cases where humans meaningfully disagree on harm judgments.

Contribution. Demonstrates that personalization significantly improves prediction of human harm judgments and reveals that imminent risks, tangible harms, and annotator traits (toxicity experience, education) systematically explain disagreement.

VLA-Arena: An Open-Source Framework for Benchmarking Vision-Language-Action Models

Problem. Quantitatively characterizing Vision-Language-Action model capability boundaries and failure modes remains challenging, with existing methods unable to systematically measure model performance across controllable difficulty axes.
Approach. VLA-Arena introduces structured task design across three orthogonal axes (Task Structure, Language Command, Visual Observation) with 11 task suites in four dimensions, 170 total tasks at fine-grained difficulty levels L0-L2, plus language and visual perturbations as diagnostic probes.

BEST: Benchmarking Efficiency in Space and Time for LLM-Generated Code

Problem. LLM-based code synthesis benchmarks evaluate time efficiency but rarely assess space efficiency, limiting comprehensive evaluation of code generation quality beyond correctness.
Approach. BEST introduces 440 expert-constructed coding tasks with subtask-based evaluation dividing each task into multiple subtasks with different input scales; proposes dual@k metric incorporating both time and space via a weighted matrix.
Contribution. Demonstrates LLMs exhibit weak time-efficiency and even weaker space-efficiency capabilities through extensive experiments across 50 LLMs; reveals critical gaps in efficient code synthesis.

QiMeng-LibBench: Benchmarking LLM Agents for Library-Scale Cross-Architecture Migration

Problem. LLM agents lack capabilities for library-scale cross-architecture code migration due to inability to disentangle library dependencies and ISA-specific kernels.
Approach. QiMeng-LibBench benchmarks LLM agents on 85 critical kernels from OpenCV, libjpeg, NCNN; proposes FSCM multi-agent framework with hardware-aware global reconfiguration and performance optimization.

Problem. Current e-commerce dialogue benchmarks lack evaluation of agents' capability to handle mixed-type conversations and complex domain rules with hallucinations.

Unpacking Human Preference for LLMs: Demographically Aware Evaluation with the HUMAINE Framework

Problem. Evaluating LLMs faces challenges: technical benchmarks lack real-world relevance and human preference studies suffer from unrepresentative sampling and superficial assessment.
Approach. HUMAINE collects multi-turn conversations from 23,404 stratified participants across 22 demographic groups to evaluate 28 models via hierarchical Bayesian Bradley-Terry-Davidson analysis.
Contribution. Reveals google/gemini-2.5-pro ranks first with 95.6% posterior probability and uncovers significant preference heterogeneity by age with 65% tie rate on abstract dimensions.

BEDTime: A Unified Benchmark for Automatically Describing Time Series

Problem. Recent multi-modal time series models claim high performance on complex reasoning tasks but skip foundational evaluations of basic ability to describe structural properties of time series.

ICML 2026

TimeLAVA: Learning-Agnostic Valuation for Time Series Data

Problem. Existing data valuation methods are model-dependent or designed for i.i.d. data, failing to capture temporal dependencies and non-stationary dynamics in time series.
Approach. TimeLAVA values temporal segments by their marginal contribution to minimizing distributional discrepancy using Selective Wavelet-based Wasserstein distance combining multi-scale wavelets with unbalanced optimal transport.
Contribution. Produces significantly more informative value scores than existing methods for anomaly detection, data pruning, and label noise detection across diverse real-world time series datasets.
wavelet transformsoptimal transportdata valuation
ICML 2026

Terminal Dimension Reduction for Time Series with Applications

Problem. Terminal embeddings for coresets cannot be linear and thus cannot directly preserve linear structures in time-series clustering.
Approach. Generalizes terminal embeddings to affine line-segments using Johnson-Lindenstrauss embeddings, enabling dimension-free coresets for clustering time-series under Fréchet distance.
Contribution. Provides first dimension-free coresets for time-series clustering with empirical results showing favorable comparison to PCA.
terminal embeddingsdimension reductiontime series clustering

Problem. Hallucination evaluation benchmarks remain fragmented and English-centric, lacking structured factual resources like knowledge graphs despite their utility for hallucination mitigation.
Approach. MultiHal bridges KG and multilinguality gaps with a KG-based multilingual benchmark mining 140k KG-paths, curated to 25.9k high-quality samples, with KG-RAG integration.
Contribution. Shows 0.12-0.36 point improvement in semantic similarity, 0.16-0.36 in NLI entailment, and 0.29-0.42 in hallucination detection with KG-RAG over vanilla QA across languages.

Approach. PRISM-Physics represents solutions as directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) of formulas encoding causal dependencies between steps, paired with rule-based symbolic formula equivalence matching for consistent validation without heuristics.

Problem. Existing web agent benchmarks measure only task completion while ignoring whether agents complete tasks safely and trustworthily for enterprise adoption.
Approach. ST-WebAgentBench provides 222 tasks paired with safety/trustworthiness (ST) policies scored along six dimensions, proposing Completion Under Policy (CuP) metric and Risk Ratio.
Contribution. Evaluation shows state-of-the-art agents have average CuP less than two-thirds of nominal completion rate, revealing substantial safety gaps.

Problem. Grounding formal logic in natural language remains fragmented, with datasets that are small-scale, domain-specific, or translate logic into overly technical forms rather than context-rich language.
Approach. Introduces VERIFY, a large-scale dataset with 200k+ rigorously generated triplets of LTL formulas, Intermediate Technical Language (ITL) bridge representations, and domain-specific NL descriptions across 13 domains.

Approach. Proposes ChronoPlay, a framework with dual-dynamic update mechanisms to track both content and community changes, and dual-source synthesis from official sources and player communities for authentic query patterns.

Problem. LLM pretraining requires both high data quantity and quality, but existing datasets struggle to balance these requirements effectively.

Train-before-Test Harmonizes Language Model Rankings

Problem. Existing language model benchmarks provide contradictory model rankings due to differences in evaluation methodology, making model selection and comparison unreliable.
Approach. Train-before-test provides each model with identical benchmark-specific fine-tuning before evaluation to compare model potential rather than out-of-the-box performance.
Contribution. Model potential rankings obtained through train-before-test exhibit remarkable consistency across all 24 benchmarks and 61 models, with strong external validity and transfer across benchmarks.

Problem. No existing speech-to-speech systems are evaluated for human-likeness, leaving unanswered whether modern S2S systems can converse like humans across paralinguistic and emotional dimensions.
Approach. The paper conducts the first Turing test for S2S systems with 2,968 human judgments, develops a fine-grained taxonomy of 18 human-likeness dimensions, and proposes an interpretable model leveraging human ratings for automatic discrimination.

AtC: Aggregate-then-Calibrate for Human-centered Assessment

Problem. Human-centered assessment tasks rely on conflicting signals: human judgments have heterogeneous expertise and inconsistent rating scales, while model scores learn from imperfect proxies.
Approach. Two-stage framework: stage-1 aggregates heterogeneous comparative judgments into a consensus ranking using a rank-aggregation model accounting for annotator reliability; stage-2 calibrates predictive model scores via isotonic projection to enforce ordinal consistency.
Contribution. Combines human judgment and model scores to outperform human-only or model-only assessments, with proven efficiency gains from accounting for annotator heterogeneity.

Approach. VisJudge-Bench is a benchmark with 3090 expert-annotated samples covering 32 chart types and dashboards, assessing MLLMs on visualization aesthetics and quality.
Contribution. Even GPT-5 shows significant gaps compared to humans with MAE of 0.553; VisJudge narrows this gap to 0.421 and increases human consistency to 0.687.

Rethinking LLM Evaluation: Can We Evaluate LLMs with 200× Less Data?

Problem. Growing benchmark suites make comprehensive LLM evaluation expensive; existing redundancy reduction methods lack systematic integration for both accuracy and ranking consistency.
Approach. EssenceBench frames benchmark compression as an optimization problem using iterative Genetic Algorithm combining fitness-based subset search and attribution-based sample search.
Contribution. Preserves model ranking with 25× fewer samples on HellaSwag and achieves 95% ranking preservation using 200× fewer samples.

Mitigating Label Shift in Tabular In-Context Learning via Test-Time Posterior Adjustment

Problem. TabPFN, a foundation model for tabular datasets using in-context learning, is vulnerable to label shift and overfits to the majority class due to imbalanced training data.

Contribution. On 250+ OpenML datasets, demonstrates substantial improvements under label shift while maintaining strong performance in standard settings without label shift.

SoftMatcha 2: A Fast and Soft Pattern Matcher for Trillion-Scale Corpora

Problem. Large-scale corpus search requires fast retrieval while handling semantic variations (substitution, insertion, deletion) in trillion-scale datasets.
Approach. SoftMatcha 2 combines suffix-array-based string matching with word embeddings and uses dynamic corpus-aware pruning and disk-aware design for scalability.
Contribution. Searches FineWeb-Edu (1.4T tokens) in under 0.3 seconds, substantially outperforming infini-gram and detects benchmark contamination existing methods miss.
suffix arrayssemantic fuzzy matchingcorpus-aware pruningdisk-aware design
ICML 2026

Scaling Behavior in Model Fine-tuning for Audio DeepFake Detection

Problem. It remains unclear how audio deepfake detection capability scales with model capacity and training data under realistic deployment conditions involving distribution shift, signal corruption, and unseen synthesis pipelines.
Approach. Present the first systematic study of scaling laws in post-training audio deepfake detection by analyzing performance, robustness, and generalization as functions of model size and training data scale.
Contribution. Reveal a fundamental asymmetry between performance scaling and robustness scaling, showing that increasing model capacity alone is insufficient for achieving reliable real-world generalization.
scaling lawsaudio deepfake detectionpost-training analysis
ICML 2026

StableI2I: Spotting Unintended Changes in Image-to-Image Transition

Problem. Image-to-image evaluation primarily focuses on instruction following and aesthetic quality but fails to assess semantic correspondence and spatial structure preservation.
Approach. StableI2I proposes a unified evaluation framework using motion-weighted loss masks to measure content fidelity and consistency without reference images, coupled with an MLLM-based benchmark.
Contribution. StableI2I provides fine-grained evaluation with strong correlation to human judgments across image editing and restoration tasks.
content fidelity measurementconsistency evaluationreference-free assessment
